  [G9.10] Material replacement-related information omitted from the recommendation
  Potential Concern — severity High
    Report:   The report addresses loss of direct fund access restriction as a safeguarded benefit that would be lost in the recommended consolidation: 'Loss of direct fund access restriction: If you were to transfer all your pensions into this plan, it would limit how you can withdraw funds. In the future, this would be important to you, as you would be unable to access this pension flexibly.' The report states no employer contributions are lost across all four replacement analyses. The report discloses that all five existing arrangements have 'No charges for transfer and/or switching' with no exit penalties.
    File:     Evidence confirms employer contributions are ongoing to People's Pension (EA1): passages P1-P8 show regular employer contributions through 2023, indicating an active workplace scheme. Zurich (EA4) received former protected rights payments totalling £2,344.58 and personal payments totalling £12,419.11 with no current regular payments being made (P11). Standard Life (EA5) policy information (P9, P16) contains no current contribution records. Scottish Widows (EA2) policy schedule (P14) makes no reference to ongoing contributions. Evidence shows People's Pension has an active employer contribution stream, contradicting the report's assertion that no employer contributions are lost. The file documents no employer contributions continue to any other arrangement. Policy documents (P11-P16) confirm no Market Value Reductions (MVRs) apply and no exit charges beyond those already disclosed. No evidence addresses whether flexible withdrawal rights or direct fund access restrictions apply to the existing arrangements or would be lost on transfer.
    Discrepancies:
      - Report states no employer contributions are lost on any of the four replacement analyses (all marked false in /replacementAnalysis[]/employerContributionsLost). Evidence shows People's Pension (EA1) has active employer contributions through at least 2023 (P1-P8), indicating this is a live workplace scheme where employer contributions would cease on transfer.
    The report correctly discloses loss of direct fund access restriction as a material feature that would be lost. However, the assertion that no employer contributions are lost is contradicted by evidence in the case file. People's Pension policy documents (P1-P8) show regular employer contributions occurring through 2023, structured as alternating employee and employer contributions with dates through September 2023. This indicates an active workplace pension scheme. The report's extracted field /replacementAnalysis[]/employerContributionsLost marks all four analyses (for the four arrangements being replaced or consolidated) as false, meaning the report asserts no employer contributions are lost. This directly contradicts the evidence that People's Pension continues to receive employer contributions. Under the guard provided ('An employer contribution that stops on leaving service is not lost by the transfer; check whether the client is still an active member'), the question is whether the client remains an active member. The evidence shows contributions are flowing through mid-2023, but the report does not explicitly state whether the client continues employment or has left the scheme. The report's silence on the loss of ongoing employer contributions to an active workplace scheme is a material omission. The advisory strategy research (P20) notes the client 'has a lot of pensions that he doesn't understand how they work and ideally wants to put them all together', indicating consolidation is the strategy. If consolidation involves transferring out of People's Pension while employed, employer contributions would cease. The report does not address this material consequence. Exit penalties are properly addressed as absent. The loss of direct fund access flexibility is addressed but no comparison is provided showing what flexibility exists in the existing arrangements versus what will be available in the new consolidated solution, limiting the ability to assess whether this feature loss is justified by other benefits.

CHK-010 — Vulnerability, Support Needs and Foreseeable Harm Overlay
Outcome: Potential Concern

  No trigger was recorded; this check applies to every case and was assessed anyway. Potential Concern across 5 requirement(s). 5 raised a concern (G10.1, G10.2, G10.3, G10.4, G10.5). Client demonstrates significant knowledge gaps (no understanding of stocks/shares, EIS/VCT, pension planning, and no prior investment experience in most categories) but report does not discuss whether these gaps affect suitability or require adjusted communication or support measures. Risk questionnaire indicated natural rating of 4 (Cautious Balanced) but client agreed to Risk 5 (Balanced) — the override is documented in the risk profile but the report does not explain whether this represents informed consent or whether additional support was provided to ensure comprehension of the risk shift. Fact Find records no preferred delivery method and no communication adjustments, yet client received advice via video call; no evidence of confirmation that video format was accessible or suitable for client's capacity. 3 cited quote(s) could not be found in the evidence supplied and were rejected.

  [G10.1] Details of additional support and needs, including third-party support
  Potential Concern — severity Moderate
    Report:   The canonical model records: thirdPartySupport.isPresent = false; estatePlanning.isPowerOfAttorneyGranted = false. The report contains no assertions about support needs, adjustments for vulnerability, communication adaptations, third-party involvement, or duty of care measures specific to this client.
    File:     The evidence reveals: (1) [P1] Risk profile questionnaire shows client answered 'No understanding / knowledge' to three investment knowledge questions (stocks/shares, EIS/VCT, pension planning) and 'No' to five investment experience questions (Unit Trusts, stocks, EIS/VCT, pensions). (2) [P5] Risk profile records: 'No additional notes were recorded' under Investor Experience and Capacity for Loss sections. (3) [P7] Fact Find shows 'No Preference' for preferred delivery method, with no recorded communication adjustments or accessibility requirements. (4) [P8] Risk profile describes agreed risk level as 'Balanced' (Risk 5) following discussion that altered the natural questionnaire result (initially 4, agreed to 5). (5) [P21] Fact Find dated 19/09/2025 records meeting type as 'Video Call - Recorded' with no additional attendees and states 'Unanswered questions or blank responses will be excluded from any advice given.' (6) [P17] Client Agreement permits third-party instructions via 'valid Power of Attorney' but contains no evidence the client has executed or granted one. (7) No passages record support needs, accessibility requirements, language preferences, literacy considerations, or special measures to aid comprehension.
    Discrepancies:
      - Client demonstrates significant knowledge gaps (no understanding of stocks/shares, EIS/VCT, pension planning, and no prior investment experience in most categories) but report does not discuss whether these gaps affect suitability or require adjusted communication or support measures.
      - Risk questionnaire indicated natural rating of 4 (Cautious Balanced) but client agreed to Risk 5 (Balanced) — the override is documented in the risk profile but the report does not explain whether this represents informed consent or whether additional support was provided to ensure comprehension of the risk shift.
      - Fact Find records no preferred delivery method and no communication adjustments, yet client received advice via video call; no evidence of confirmation that video format was accessible or suitable for client's capacity.
      - Risk profile section 'Capacity for Loss' and 'Investor Experience' sections both state 'No additional notes were recorded,' indicating no assessment or discussion of any support needs or comprehension requirements.
    The report asserts no third-party support (isPresent = false) and no Power of Attorney (isPowerOfAttorneyGranted = false). These negative assertions are consistent with the file: no document records current third-party support, and [P17] only describes the mechanism by which such support could be authorised in future, not that it has been implemented. However, the comparison reveals a material gap. The client exhibits clear low-capability characteristics under FCA guidance FG21/1: (1) No understanding of core investment concepts (stocks, EIS/VCT, pensions) per [P1]; (2) No prior investment experience in unit trusts, stocks, or pension products per [P1]; (3) Natural attitude to risk questionnaire result (Risk 4, Cautious Balanced) overridden to Risk 5 (Balanced) per [P8], [P11] — this is a material shift upward from the psychometric baseline. The risk profile [P8] explains that 'The natural risk level...is used as a starting point to discuss the actual level of risk you are willing and able to take' and confirms 'we came to an agreed risk level for your specific investment objective,' but [P9] records 'No additional notes were recorded' under Agreed Risk Level, providing no narrative of how the client's comprehension of the higher-risk outcome was ensured or what support was provided. The Capacity for Loss questionnaire section [P5] similarly records 'No additional notes were recorded,' meaning no documented assessment of the client's ability to bear loss or need for protective measures. The fact that knowledge gaps exist alongside a risk upgrade, and no supporting assessment of comprehension or capacity is recorded, creates a process risk: the report does not reflect consideration of whether the client's low knowledge and experience justified adjusted communication, simplified materials, or documented confirmation of understanding before proceeding with a recommendation to higher-risk investments. The report's silence on this overlay is not explained by the evidence — it is a gap in the evidence itself. The guards distinguish future planning (PoA recommendations) from current support (this check concerns current arrangements), and they note that spouse presence does not constitute vulnerability support. Neither guard applies: there is no PoA recommendation here, and no spouse is recorded as present. The comparison can be performed: the report correctly states no current third-party support and no PoA. But the requirement is not limited to assertions of current support — it extends to whether 'information relating to client vulnerabilities, support needs, communication preferences, capacity, comprehension requirements or duty of care considerations' is 'fully supported by the evidence provided.' The evidence shows low capability indicators (no investment knowledge, no experience, risk rating override without recorded justification). The report provides no corresponding documentation of vulnerability assessment, support needs, communication adjustments, or duty of care measures. This is a Potential Concern: material evidence of low-capability drivers exists in the file, but the report does not reflect consideration of support, communication, or comprehension measures that would be appropriate under PRIN 2A and FG21/1 for a client with these characteristics.

  [G10.2] Communication preferences and formats
  Potential Concern — severity Low
    Report:   The suitability report (category I) contains no explicit statement of the client's communication preferences, accessible formats, language requirements, or literacy considerations. The canonical model holds no value at the path `/vulnerability/perClient[]/communicationPreferences`.
    File:     The Fact Find (category B, P9) records 'Preferred delivery method: No Preference'. The TFAS Wealth Client Agreement Privacy (category A, P1-P5) references that clients may communicate with the firm by 'post, phone or e-mail, or in any other way a member feels is appropriate' (P9), but this is a general service description, not a client-specific preference. The Terms and Conditions (category H, P24) state 'You may change Your address, email and other personal details either by contacting Us or through Our Online Service or via Your Adviser' and notes that where a chosen method of communication is email, clients receive notifications via email or through an online portal. However, no specific communication preference for this client is documented beyond the Fact Find's 'No Preference' entry.
    Discrepancies:
      - The suitability report makes no reference to communication preferences at all, while the Fact Find records 'No Preference' as the client's stated delivery method. This is not a contradiction but a silence: the report does not acknowledge, record or reflect what the Fact Find captured.
      - No client-specific accessible formats, language requirements, or literacy considerations are mentioned in either the report or the file.
    The comparison can be made. The Fact Find explicitly captures communication preference as 'No Preference' (P9). This is a factual data point: the client was asked and indicated no specific preference. The suitability report does not reference this at all—neither the question nor the answer appears anywhere in its text (P8, P10-P22). This is not an inconsistency in the data itself; both sides agree 'no preference' was the answer. However, the requirement asks whether the report 'documents and reflects' the client's communication preferences. The report does not document them—it is silent. The FCA handbook (PRIN 2A.2.1R, PRIN 2A.5, PRIN 2A.6) and FG21/1 require firms to communicate with clients in a way that is clear, fair and not misleading, and to take account of clients' characteristics and circumstances, including any vulnerability or support needs. Where a firm has asked about communication preferences and received a response—even 'no preference'—good practice under PRIN 2A involves acknowledging and recording this as part of the case documentation. The absence of any reference to this captured data in the suitability report itself represents a process gap: the report does not discharge the transparency obligation to explain how the firm will communicate with the client or why the stated preference (or lack thereof) does not affect suitability. The report is silent on a material point of client service delivery. The guard notes that a standard contact preference is not a vulnerability adjustment and should not be treated as evidence that vulnerability was assessed; this applies here, and no vulnerability is flagged. However, the silence itself—the failure to acknowledge and reflect even a straightforward 'no preference' in the primary advice document—falls short of the requirement to document and reflect communication preferences. This is a disclosure shortfall and a process risk.

        "You may change Your address, email and other personal details either by contacting Us or through Our Online Service or via Your Adviser."

  [G10.3] Adjustments made to support client comprehension
  Potential Concern — severity Moderate — citations partly verified
    Report:   The suitability report does not assert any adjustments made to support client comprehension. The canonical model contains no values at `/vulnerability/perClient[]/adjustmentsMade[]` or `/vulnerability/perClient[]/characteristics[]`. The report does reference communication preferences: the Fact Find [P2] records 'No Preference' for preferred delivery method and confirms consent to contact 'by post, phone or e-mail, or in any other way a member feels is appropriate'. The report itself [P1] states 'If there are any points on which you are unsure, or require further clarification, please contact me and I will be pleased to explain these in greater detail' and [P4] notes 'Access to a professional, experienced and qualified team of financial advisers at any time to provide ad-hoc advice as required.' However, no specific adjustments to support comprehension—such as accessible formats, plain language explanations, visual aids, simplified documentation, or arranged interpreter or reader support—are documented in the report.
    File:     The case file contains: (1) A Fact Find [P2] recording marketing preferences and delivery method as 'No Preference' with standard consent language for data processing. (2) File notes [P11], [P12] recording the adviser's working notes on attitude to risk and capacity for loss discussions, including narrative descriptions of the client's understanding and risk appetite. (3) A Risk profile report [P19], [P23] with no additional notes recorded on investor experience or capacity for loss questionnaire discussions. (4) The Fact Find [P8] showing a monthly cash flow deficit of £-288.00 with no indication of whether this was discussed with the client or how the client's comprehension of this negative position was supported. (5) The report [P5] recording that the client 'confirmed' various points ('you confirmed that any potential underperformance would not have a detrimental impact on your day-to-day living'), suggesting acknowledgment but not recording any steps taken to verify understanding or adapt communication. No document records any assessment of the client's literacy, language, sensory, cognitive, or communication access needs.
    Discrepancies:
      - The report asserts no adjustments to support comprehension, while the file contains indications that comprehension should have been verified (e.g., confirmations of understanding in [P5]). This is an absence of assertion against partial evidence of discussion, not a direct contradiction.
      - The Fact Find [P2] records 'No Preference' for delivery method, which could mean no special adjustment was needed or that no preference was recorded. The report does not clarify whether this means the client's needs were assessed and found to require no adjustment, or whether no such assessment occurred.
      - File notes [P11], [P12] contain narrative descriptions of client understanding but no record of adjustments made to facilitate that understanding.
      - The report [P5] records client confirmations but does not document what explanations, materials, or support methods were used to enable those confirmations.
    NOT ASSESSED — the comparison needs a value that was not available: Whether any vulnerability, support need, communication difficulty, literacy concern, language barrier, sensory access requirement, cognitive capacity limitation, or other comprehension driver was identified for this client., What adjustments, if any, were made to communication, documentation format, explanation method, or third-party involvement to support the client's understanding., Whether the client's capacity to understand the advice, its risks, costs, and implications was formally assessed., What evidence exists that the client understood the recommendation before accepting it, beyond recorded confirmations.
    The comparison cannot be performed because the report makes no assertion about adjustments made to support comprehension, and the canonical model extraction found no values at the vulnerability paths. The file contains partial evidence of discussion (confirmations recorded in [P5], working notes in [P11], [P12]) but no clear record of vulnerabilities identified or adjustments implemented. The Fact Find [P2] records 'No Preference' for delivery method, which is neutral—it neither confirms that comprehension support was assessed and found unnecessary, nor that no assessment occurred. The absence of any documented vulnerability assessment, communication access needs evaluation, or documented adjustments to support understanding in a case where the client has a negative monthly cash flow (£-288.00, [P8]), is working as an HGV driver, and has confirmed understanding of complex pension consolidation and investment switching, raises a process risk: there is no evidence that the report's narrative descriptions of the client's understanding (e.g., [P5] 'you confirmed...', [P11] 'You confirmed...') were preceded by any recorded steps to verify comprehension or adapt communication to the client's needs. The guard specifies that standard cancellation rights are a regulatory feature, not an adjustment made for this client—this does not apply here. The key finding is not a contradiction between report and file, but the absence of documented evidence in either location that comprehension support was assessed, planned, or delivered.

  [G10.4] Vulnerability has a material impact on the recommendation (overlay)
  Potential Concern — severity High — citations partly verified
    Report:   The suitability report contains no extracted values at paths /vulnerability/perClient[]/impactOnRecommendation[] or /vulnerability/foreseeableHarms[]. The report does not assert any vulnerability, does not describe any support needs arising from vulnerability, and does not explain how vulnerability (if present) affects the suitability of the recommendation.
    File:     The case file contains evidence of multiple vulnerability indicators: (1) Client demonstrates very limited financial knowledge and investment understanding—passage [P1] shows responses of 'No understanding / knowledge' to questions about stocks and shares, enterprise schemes, venture capital trusts, and pension planning. The client has no investment experience in unit trusts, stocks and shares, or pension products. (2) Passage [P2] records 'No additional notes were recorded' in both Investor Experience and Capacity for Loss sections, indicating a potential assessment gap. (3) Passage [P14] describes the client's attitude to risk and capacity for loss: the client has 'limited' experience confined to 'workplace pension arrangements' and is 'not reliant upon pension funds'. (4) Passage [P6] records the client's stated need: 'Just needs advice helping him organise his pensions in the best place. Wants to consolidate to help him get his head around what he has.' This language suggests cognitive or organizational difficulty. (5) Passage [P3] and [P10] show the client's natural risk rating was 4 (Cautious Balanced) but was overridden to 5 (Balanced), confirmed in passage [P14]: 'You confirmed that you wish to increase your Risk rating to 5'. (6) Passage [P14] confirms capacity for loss: 'any potential underperformance would not have a detrimental impact on your day-to-day living' due to state pension and employment income. (7) Passage [P20] recommends 'It would also be prudent to set up and register Power of Attorney for your financial affairs / health and welfare, to ensure that in the event of your incapacity, your spouse or relative could act on your behalf'—flagging incapacity risk. (8) Passage [P12] shows current monthly disposable income is negative (£-288.00 monthly shortfall), indicating financial strain.
    Discrepancies:
      - Report asserts no vulnerability or support needs; file contains multiple indicators of limited financial knowledge, investment inexperience, and need for cognitive support to 'get his head around' his pensions.
      - Report does not reflect the client's very limited understanding of stocks, shares, pension planning, or investment risk (evidenced in [P1]).
      - Report does not reference or assess the gaps in Investor Experience or Capacity for Loss notes (both blank in [P2]), suggesting assessment may not have been documented.
      - Report overrides client's natural risk rating from 4 to 5 without explaining how this aligns with the client's stated limited understanding or need for consolidation support.
      - Report recommends Power of Attorney ([P20]) as a precaution but does not explain what vulnerability or incapacity concern this addresses.
      - Report does not assess whether the client's negative monthly cash flow (£-288 shortfall per [P12]) affects capacity to sustain the investment or creates vulnerability.
    The comparison can be performed because the report's silence is explicit—the canonical model holds no values for vulnerability impact or foreseen harms—and the file contains clear evidence of circumstances that trigger FG21/1 vulnerability assessment obligations. The client exhibits multiple characteristics that should trigger vulnerability assessment: (1) Low financial capability—'No understanding / knowledge' across stocks, shares, pensions, and investment risk; (2) Limited investment experience—confined to workplace pension and cash accounts only; (3) Cognitive or organizational support need—stated as wanting help to 'get his head around what he has'; (4) Financial strain—negative monthly cash flow of £288 shortfall; (5) Age—69 years old, at life transition point (retirement in 2 years per [P6]); (6) Incapacity risk—report itself recommends Power of Attorney. These are classic FG21/1 drivers (low capability, life event, financial resilience concern, health/incapacity planning). The report overrides the client's natural cautious risk rating (4) to balanced (5) without addressing how this sits with his limited knowledge or consolidation need. Passage [P2] shows blank assessment notes for Investor Experience and Capacity for Loss, suggesting these sections were not engaged with meaningfully. Passage [P6] records the client's goal as consolidation and cognitive clarity, not growth. Passage [P14] confirms the client understands funds 'may go up as well as down' and is 'not reliant upon pension funds', yet does not explain why moving from a cautious to balanced risk profile aligns with someone of limited knowledge facing retirement. The critical issue is not whether vulnerability exists—the file strongly suggests it does—but that the report does not acknowledge it, assess it, or explain how the recommendation accounts for it. Where a report asserts no vulnerability but the file contains clear FG21/1 drivers and no documented assessment, the overlay assessment is unmet. The report's recommendation to consolidate pensions into a new Aviva platform with balanced-rated funds may be appropriate for a resilient client with investment knowledge, but no assessment demonstrates it is appropriate for this client's circumstances.

  [G10.5] Vulnerabilities recorded in the file and in the report
  Potential Concern — severity Moderate — citations partly verified
    Report:   The suitability report states hasVulnerability = No. No vulnerability categories, characteristics, or assessedOn date are present in the extracted canonical model. The report is silent on vulnerability assessment outcomes, though it does discuss the client's attitude to risk, capacity for loss, income stability, and financial position in narrative form.
    File:     The file contains multiple passages addressing vulnerability-adjacent matters: (1) [P1] discusses ATR and capacity for loss, stating the client 'confirmed that any potential underperformance would not have a detrimental impact on your day-to-day living' and noting income from employment (£300/week net as HGV driver, £20/week Sea Cadets, spouse contributing £1,100/month), plus state pension of £230/week. (2) [P3], [P4], [P10], [P13] are the fact find and risk profile documents, which record 'No additional notes were recorded' under Investor Experience and Capacity for Loss questionnaire sections. (3) [P8], [P9] show the client's monthly cash flow: net monthly income £1,430, monthly expenditure £1,718, resulting in a monthly disposable income of -£288. (4) [P19] projects illness and death events at age 100. (5) The file does not contain any explicit statement of a formal vulnerability assessment, recorded vulnerabilities, support needs, Power of Attorney, trusted contacts, communication adjustments, or accessibility requirements.
    Discrepancies:
      - The report asserts no vulnerability (hasVulnerability = No) but does not document a formal vulnerability assessment process or outcome in the extracted model. The file notes [P1] discuss capacity for loss and financial resilience but no explicit vulnerability assessment record is present.
      - The cash flow analysis [P8], [P9] shows a negative monthly disposable income of -£288, which could indicate financial stress or vulnerability drivers, but the report does not reference this as a vulnerability consideration or as a limitation on suitability.
      - [P13] records 'No additional notes were recorded' under both Investor Experience and Capacity for Loss questionnaire sections, which suggests these sections were not populated with detail, yet the report asserts a capacity for loss finding without explaining what basis that finding rests on beyond the bare narrative in [P1].
      - The client is aged 69 (born 07/06/1956) per [P3], approaching or at state pension age (66 per [P19]), and the fact find [P4] shows no stated accessibility requirements or communication adjustments beyond email marketing preference [P10], yet the report does not document whether age, retirement imminence, or communication capability were assessed as vulnerability drivers.
    The canonical model extraction shows hasVulnerability = No but does not populate the supporting fields (/vulnerability/perClient[]/categories[], /vulnerability/perClient[]/characteristics[], /vulnerability/perClient[]/assessedOn). The extraction report indicates these are 'absent from the extracted model' but does not state whether this is due to extraction failure or report silence. On the evidence supplied: (1) The file notes [P1] contain a capacity for loss discussion and confirm the client's understanding of risk and lack of dependence on pension income for day-to-day living. (2) The fact find [P3], [P4], [P13] show fields for vulnerability-related information (Investor Experience, Capacity for Loss Questionnaire) but these record 'No additional notes were recorded', suggesting the questionnaire sections were completed but not annotated. (3) The cash flow [P8], [P9] reveals a negative monthly disposable income of -£288, which represents a material financial resilience concern: the client's stated outgoings exceed stated income. However, the context provided in [P1] indicates the spouse (Kim) contributes £1,100 per month, which if combined would create a joint household surplus, but the report addresses this client in singular form and does not articulate how the household cash flow reconciles. (4) No formal vulnerability screening tool outcome, Power of Attorney, trusted contact, communication adjustment, or accessibility requirement is recorded anywhere in the file. (5) The report's assertion of 'no vulnerability' is internally consistent with the absence of recorded vulnerabilities in the file, but the negative personal cash flow (£-288/month) and the reliance on spouse income to sustain household expenditure represent potential vulnerability drivers that do not appear to have been formally assessed, categorised, or documented as either present or excluded. (6) The guards state that 'a vulnerability the client asked not to have written into the report may still have been handled correctly; read the file notes before reporting silence as a gap' and 'age alone is not vulnerability'. The file notes do not show a request to exclude vulnerability findings, and the narrative in [P1] does discuss capacity and financial resilience, but a formal vulnerability assessment record is absent from both the report and the file. (7) The absence of an explicit vulnerability assessment process is itself a gap: under PRIN 2A.5 and FCA FG21/1, advisers must assess client vulnerability, particularly where financial resilience is tested (as here, by negative personal cash flow), and document that assessment. The report's silence on how this assessment was conducted, and on whether the negative cash flow was considered a vulnerability indicator, is a material omission.
